# PR Summary: Fix JSON Serialization Roundtrip for WhatsApp Device Storage

## Problem
The `key_pair_serde` deserialization function had a critical bug preventing WhatsApp session persistence. When serializing bytes, `serde_json` creates an integer array `[1, 2, 3, ...]`. However, deserialization expected a base64-encoded string instead, causing runtime failures: `"invalid type: sequence, expected a borrowed byte array"`. This meant sessions couldn't survive process restarts.

## Solution
The fix changes the deserialization target type from `&[u8]` (borrowed slice) to `Vec<u8>` (owned vector). The `Vec<u8>` implementation correctly uses `visit_seq`, which accepts JSON integer arrays, whereas the borrowed slice variant expects base64 strings.

## Key Code Changes
The modification occurs in `wacore/src/store/device.rs` in the `key_pair_serde::deserialize` function—replacing the borrowed byte slice parameter with an owned vector. Additionally, a regression test `test_device_serde_roundtrip` was added to verify that Device structures survive serialization-deserialization cycles, catching this type of bug in the future.

## Impact
This resolves a blocker preventing sled-backed WhatsApp stores (like `moltis-whatsapp`) from functioning after restarts, which previously required complete re-pairing.
```

> AGENT

Here's my analysis:

**The whatsapp-rust fix (PR #311) is the actual root cause fix.** The error in issue #274 is:

> `invalid type: sequence, expected a borrowed byte array`

This is exactly the bug in `wacore/src/store/device.rs` — `key_pair_serde::deserialize` uses `&[u8]` (expects base64 strings) but `serde_json` serializes bytes as integer arrays `[1, 2, 3, ...]`. PR #311 fixes this by switching to `Vec<u8>` which correctly handles `visit_seq`.

**PR #285 fixes a different problem** — sled lock contention (`WouldBlock`) during stop/start cycles. Its description even says it fixes `"failed to open sled store ... could not acquire lock ... WouldBlock"`, which is a separate error from the deserialization one in the issue. PR #285 may address a real problem, but it doesn't fix the root cause of #274.

**What's needed:**
1. **whatsapp-rust PR #311 must be merged and released** — this is the essential fix. We're on `whatsapp-rust 0.2.0`; once a new version ships with the fix, we bump the dependency.
2. **PR #285 is orthogonal** — it addresses lifecycle/locking issues that may be worth having, but it won't resolve the deserialization error on its own. It should be reviewed on its own merits, not as a fix for #274.

**TL;DR**: Just the whatsapp-rust fix (PR #311) is enough to resolve issue #274. PR #285 tackles a separate locking concern.

+fn mirror_connected(accounts: &AccountStateMap, account_id: &str, connected: bool) {
+    let mut map = accounts.write().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
+    if let Some(state) = map.get_mut(account_id) {
+        state
+            .connected
+            .store(connected, Ordering::Relaxed);
+    }
+}
+
+fn mirror_latest_qr(accounts: &AccountStateMap, account_id: &str, qr: Option<String>) {
+    let mut map = accounts.write().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
+    if let Some(state) = map.get_mut(account_id)
+        && let Ok(mut latest_qr) = state.latest_qr.write()
+    {
+        *latest_qr = qr;
+    }
+}
+
+fn current_config(
+    accounts: &AccountStateMap,
+    account_id: &str,
+    fallback: &WhatsAppAccountConfig,
+) -> WhatsAppAccountConfig {
+    let map = accounts.read().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
+    map.get(account_id)
+        .map(|s| s.config.clone())
+        .unwrap_or_else(|| fallback.clone())
+}
+
 /// Process an incoming whatsapp-rust event for the given account.

+fn postcard_err(e: postcard::Error) -> StoreError {
+    StoreError::Serialization(e.to_string())
+}
+
+fn encode_persistent<T: Serialize>(value: &T) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {
+    postcard::to_allocvec(value).map_err(postcard_err)
+}
+
+fn decode_persistent<T: DeserializeOwned>(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<T> {
+    match postcard::from_bytes::<T>(bytes) {
+        Ok(value) => Ok(value),
+        Err(_) => serde_json::from_slice(bytes).map_err(json_err),
+    }
+}

 /// Shared account state map.
 pub type AccountStateMap = Arc<RwLock<HashMap<String, AccountState>>>;
 
+/// Synchronization primitive for graceful bot shutdown.
+pub struct ShutdownState {
+    done: AtomicBool,
+    notify: Notify,
+}
+
+impl Default for ShutdownState {
+    fn default() -> Self {
+        Self::new()
+    }
+}
+
+impl ShutdownState {
+    pub fn new() -> Self {
+        Self {
+            done: AtomicBool::new(false),
+            notify: Notify::new(),
+        }
+    }
+
+    pub fn is_done(&self) -> bool {
+        self.done.load(Ordering::Acquire)
+    }
+
+    pub fn mark_done(&self) {
+        self.done.store(true, Ordering::Release);
+        self.notify.notify_waiters();
+    }
+
+    pub async fn wait(&self) {
+        while !self.is_done() {
+            self.notify.notified().await;
+        }
+    }
+}
+
 /// Per-account runtime state.
 pub struct AccountState {
     pub client: Arc<Client>,
     pub account_id: String,
     pub config: WhatsAppAccountConfig,
     pub cancel: CancellationToken,
+    pub shutdown: Arc<ShutdownState>,
     pub message_log: Option<Arc<dyn MessageLog>>,
     pub event_sink: Option<Arc<dyn ChannelEventSink>>,
     /// Latest QR code data for the pairing flow (updated every ~20s).
     pub latest_qr: RwLock<Option<String>>,
     /// Whether the client is currently connected.
-    pub connected: std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool,
+    pub connected: AtomicBool,
     /// In-memory OTP challenges for self-approval (std::sync::Mutex because
     /// all OTP operations are synchronous HashMap lookups, never held across
     /// `.await` points).
@@ -210,4 +254,20 @@ mod tests {
         assert!(msg.conversation.is_none());
         assert!(msg.extended_text_message.is_none());
     }
+
+    #[tokio::test]
+    async fn shutdown_state_waits_for_completion() {
+        let shutdown = Arc::new(ShutdownState::new());
+        let wait_state = Arc::clone(&shutdown);
+
+        let waiter = tokio::spawn(async move {
+            wait_state.wait().await;
+        });
+
+        tokio::task::yield_now().await;
+        assert!(!shutdown.is_done());
+        shutdown.mark_done();
+        waiter.await.unwrap();
+        assert!(shutdown.is_done());

> AGENT

Now I understand the full picture. The PR's change to `channel.rs` was based on the old code with `self.stop_plugin_account()` / `self.start_plugin_account()` / `self.hot_update_config()`. On main, those were replaced by `self.registry.*` calls. The PR wanted to special-case WhatsApp to use `hot_update_config` instead of stop+start to avoid sled lock races.

The equivalent fix on main needs to use `self.registry.update_account_config()` for WhatsApp instead of stop+start. Let me check if that method exists.

> AGENT

Conflict resolved and compiles. The merge is staged and ready. Here's the summary:

**Conflict**: Only in `crates/gateway/src/channel.rs` — the `update()` method.

**Root cause**: Main refactored all channel dispatch from individual plugin fields (`self.telegram`, `self.whatsapp`, etc.) to a unified `self.registry` pattern. The PR was written against the old API.

**Resolution**: Applied the PR's WhatsApp special-casing logic using the new registry API:
- **WhatsApp**: calls `self.registry.update_account_config()` (hot update, avoids sled lock race)
- **All others**: stop + start via `self.registry.stop_account()` / `self.registry.start_account()` (existing behavior)

The merge is staged but not committed. Want me to commit it?

---
File: crates/whatsapp/src/state.rs:68
Author: greptile-apps[bot]
Body: **Race condition in `ShutdownState::wait()` with `notify_waiters()`**

`tokio::sync::Notify::notify_waiters()` is *transient* — it only wakes tasks that are already suspended at `.notified().await`. If `mark_done()` fires after the `!self.is_done()` guard passes but before the task reaches `.notified().await`, the notification is silently dropped and `wait()` blocks until the 10-second timeout in `stop_account()` expires.

Concrete race:
1. `wait()` evaluates `!self.is_done()` → `true` (not yet done)
2. Tokio scheduler runs the bot task to completion → `mark_done()` stores `true` and calls `notify_waiters()` (no waiters registered yet, notification lost)
3. `wait()` finally suspends at `self.notify.notified().await` → hangs indefinitely until the 10-second timeout

The fix is to subscribe to the `Notified` future *before* checking `is_done()`:

```rust
pub async fn wait(&self) {
    loop {
        let notified = self.notify.notified();
        if self.is_done() {
            return;
        }
        notified.await;
    }
}
```

Alternatively, replace `notify_waiters()` with `notify_one()` in `mark_done()` (which stores a permit and can be consumed by a future waiter), or use a `tokio::sync::watch` channel which does not have this race.

---
File: crates/gateway/src/channel.rs:547
Author: greptile-apps[bot]
Body: **`update()` silently succeeds when WhatsApp in-memory config update fails**

`hot_update_config` discards its error result (only emitting a `warn!` log). This means the `update()` API returns `Ok({"updated": ...})` to the caller even when the in-memory config was never applied — for example, if the WhatsApp account has crashed or is not running.

The config is correctly persisted to the store, so a subsequent restart will pick it up, but the caller has no indication that the live session was not updated. Any subsequent access-control, allowlist, or model decisions continue using stale in-memory config until the bot restarts.

Consider propagating the error — either return a partial-success body (`{"updated": ..., "warning": "config applied to store but not to live session"}`) or elevate the warning to an error for the HTTP response so operators are alerted.

---
File: crates/whatsapp/src/sled_store.rs:71
Author: greptile-apps[bot]
Body: **Postcard migration is irreversible — no format discriminator stored**

New data is encoded with `postcard` and read back via a try-postcard-then-JSON fallback. This silently migrates each key the first time it is rewritten, but there is no per-record format tag or schema version. Consequences:

- **Rollback risk**: If the deployment is rolled back to the previous binary (which only knows JSON), every record that was already rewritten in postcard format will fail both the postcard *and* JSON parse, causing deserialization errors at runtime for critical session state (keys, device records, LID mappings).
- **Silent misparse risk**: `postcard::from_bytes` does not check any magic header. In pathological cases (e.g. if some legacy JSON bytes happen to be valid postcard), data could be silently decoded with the wrong type layout.

Consider storing a one-byte format tag (`0x00` = JSON, `0x01` = postcard) as a prefix so a rollback can detect and skip postcard-encoded entries, or at minimum document the deployment constraint that rollback requires a data wipe / separate migration step.
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/whatsapp-fail/crates/whatsapp/src/sled_store.rs",
  "old_string": "fn postcard_err(e: postcard::Error) -> StoreError {\n    StoreError::Serialization(e.to_string())\n}\n\nfn encode_persistent<T: Serialize>(value: &T) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {\n    postcard::to_allocvec(value).map_err(postcard_err)\n}\n\nfn decode_persistent<T: DeserializeOwned>(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<T> {\n    match postcard::from_bytes::<T>(bytes) {\n        Ok(value) => Ok(value),\n        Err(_) => serde_json::from_slice(bytes).map_err(json_err),\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "fn postcard_err(e: postcard::Error) -> StoreError {\n    StoreError::Serialization(e.to_string())\n}\n\n/// Format tag prefixed to every encoded record.\nconst FORMAT_POSTCARD: u8 = 0x01;\n\nfn encode_persistent<T: Serialize>(value: &T) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {\n    let body = postcard::to_allocvec(value).map_err(postcard_err)?;\n    let mut buf = Vec::with_capacity(1 + body.len());\n    buf.push(FORMAT_POSTCARD);\n    buf.extend_from_slice(&body);\n    Ok(buf)\n}\n\nfn decode_persistent<T: DeserializeOwned>(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<T> {\n    if bytes.first() == Some(&FORMAT_POSTCARD) {\n        return postcard::from_bytes::<T>(&bytes[1..]).map_err(postcard_err);\n    }\n    // Legacy: untagged data is JSON (pre-postcard migration).\n    serde_json::from_slice(bytes).map_err(json_err)\n}"
}

> AGENT

All three Greptile review issues are fixed and all 67 whatsapp tests pass:

1. **`ShutdownState::wait()` race** — `notified()` future is now created before the `is_done()` check, so the notification can't be lost between check and await.

2. **Silent `update()` success** — Response now includes `"warning": "config saved to store but live session was not updated"` when the hot-update fails, so callers aren't misled.

3. **Postcard format tag** — `encode_persistent` prefixes `0x01` before postcard bytes; `decode_persistent` dispatches on the tag and falls back to JSON only for untagged legacy data. Rollbacks will fail fast instead of silently misparsing.

> AGENT

fmt, i18n, and zizmor all pass now. The only failure is `biome: command not found` — that's a local toolchain issue (biome isn't installed in this shell's PATH), not related to our changes. There are no JS changes in this PR anyway.

Summary of what was fixed:

1. **Conflict resolution** in `channel.rs` — adapted PR's WhatsApp hot-update logic to use the new `self.registry` API
2. **Greptile fixes**: `ShutdownState::wait()` race condition, `update()` silent success, postcard format tag
3. **Pre-existing syntax error** in `auth_middleware.rs:326` — missing `.send().await.unwrap()`
4. **rustfmt** — ran nightly formatter on all merged files

   393→/// Generate a systemd user unit file.
   394→pub fn generate_systemd_unit(
   395→    moltis_bin: &Path,
   396→    _config: &ServiceConfig,
   397→    log_path: &Path,
   398→) -> String {
   399→    let bin = moltis_bin.display();
   400→    let log = log_path.display();
   401→
   402→    let exec_args = format!("{bin} node run");
   403→
   404→    format!(
   405→        r#"[Unit]
   406→Description=Moltis Node Host
   407→After=network-online.target
   408→Wants=network-online.target
   409→
   410→[Service]
   411→Type=simple
   412→ExecStart={exec_args}
   413→Restart=on-failure
   414→RestartSec=10
   415→StandardOutput=append:{log}
   416→StandardError=append:{log}
   417→Environment=RUST_LOG=info
   418→
   419→[Install]
   420→WantedBy=default.target
   421→"#
   422→    )
   423→}
   424→
   425→fn install_systemd(
   426→    moltis_bin: &Path,
   427→    config: &ServiceConfig,
   428→    log_path: &Path,
   429→) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
   430→    let unit_path = systemd_unit_path()?;
   431→
   432→    // Stop if already running (ignore errors).
   433→    let _ = Command::new("systemctl")
   434→        .args(["--user", "stop", SYSTEMD_UNIT])
   435→        .output();
   436→
   437→    let unit = generate_systemd_unit(moltis_bin, config, log_path);
   438→
   439→    if let Some(parent) = unit_path.parent() {
   440→        fs::create_dir_all(parent)?;
   441→    }
   442→    fs::write(&unit_path, &unit)?;
   443→    info!(path = %unit_path.display(), "wrote systemd unit");
   444→
   445→    // Reload, enable, start.
   446→    run_systemctl(&["daemon-reload"])?;
   447→    run_systemctl(&["enable", SYSTEMD_UNIT])?;
   448→    run_systemctl(&["start", SYSTEMD_UNIT])?;
   449→
   450→    info!("node service installed and started");
   451→    Ok(())

> AGENT

The service runs via `moltis node run` which loads `ServiceConfig` from `node.json` in the data dir. So the plist/systemd unit should pass environment variables like `MOLTIS_GATEWAY_URL`, `MOLTIS_DEVICE_TOKEN`, etc., or pass config via CLI args. Looking at the test assertions more carefully:

- The launchd test asserts `plist.contains("ws://gw:9090/ws")` — config values as env vars in the plist
- The "omits" tests check for `--node-id`, `--name`, `--working-dir` — CLI args pattern

So the config should be added as environment variables in the plist `<dict>` and as `Environment=` lines in the systemd unit. Let me implement this properly.

> TOOL

  "old_string": "/// Generate a launchd plist XML string.\npub fn generate_launchd_plist(\n    moltis_bin: &Path,\n    _config: &ServiceConfig,\n    log_path: &Path,\n) -> String {\n    let bin = moltis_bin.display();\n    let log = log_path.display();\n\n    let args = [\n        format!(\"    <string>{bin}</string>\"),\n        \"    <string>node</string>\".to_string(),\n        \"    <string>run</string>\".to_string(),\n    ];\n\n    let args_str = args.join(\"\\n\");\n\n    format!(\n        r#\"<?xml version=\"1.0\" encoding=\"UTF-8\"?>\n<!DOCTYPE plist PUBLIC \"-//Apple//DTD PLIST 1.0//EN\" \"http://www.apple.com/DTDs/PropertyList-1.0.dtd\">\n<plist version=\"1.0\">\n<dict>\n  <key>Label</key>\n  <string>{LAUNCHD_LABEL}</string>\n  <key>ProgramArguments</key>\n  <array>\n{args_str}\n  </array>\n  <key>RunAtLoad</key>\n  <true/>\n  <key>KeepAlive</key>\n  <dict>\n    <key>SuccessfulExit</key>\n    <false/>\n  </dict>\n  <key>ThrottleInterval</key>\n  <integer>10</integer>\n  <key>StandardOutPath</key>\n  <string>{log}</string>\n  <key>StandardErrorPath</key>\n  <string>{log}</string>\n  <key>ProcessType</key>\n  <string>Background</string>\n</dict>\n</plist>\n\"#\n    )\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Generate a launchd plist XML string.\npub fn generate_launchd_plist(\n    moltis_bin: &Path,\n    config: &ServiceConfig,\n    log_path: &Path,\n) -> String {\n    let bin = moltis_bin.display();\n    let log = log_path.display();\n\n    let mut args = vec![\n        format!(\"    <string>{bin}</string>\"),\n        \"    <string>node</string>\".to_string(),\n        \"    <string>run</string>\".to_string(),\n        format!(\"    <string>--gateway-url</string>\\n    <string>{}</string>\", config.gateway_url),\n        format!(\"    <string>--device-token</string>\\n    <string>{}</string>\", config.device_token),\n        format!(\"    <string>--timeout</string>\\n    <string>{}</string>\", config.timeout),\n    ];\n    if let Some(ref id) = config.node_id {\n        args.push(format!(\n            \"    <string>--node-id</string>\\n    <string>{id}</string>\"\n        ));\n    }\n    if let Some(ref name) = config.display_name {\n        args.push(format!(\n            \"    <string>--name</string>\\n    <string>{name}</string>\"\n        ));\n    }\n    if let Some(ref dir) = config.working_dir {\n        args.push(format!(\n            \"    <string>--working-dir</string>\\n    <string>{dir}</string>\"\n        ));\n    }\n\n    let args_str = args.join(\"\\n\");\n\n    format!(\n        r#\"<?xml version=\"1.0\" encoding=\"UTF-8\"?>\n<!DOCTYPE plist PUBLIC \"-//Apple//DTD PLIST 1.0//EN\" \"http://www.apple.com/DTDs/PropertyList-1.0.dtd\">\n<plist version=\"1.0\">\n<dict>\n  <key>Label</key>\n  <string>{LAUNCHD_LABEL}</string>\n  <key>ProgramArguments</key>\n  <array>\n{args_str}\n  </array>\n  <key>RunAtLoad</key>\n  <true/>\n  <key>KeepAlive</key>\n  <dict>\n    <key>SuccessfulExit</key>\n    <false/>\n  </dict>\n  <key>ThrottleInterval</key>\n  <integer>10</integer>\n  <key>StandardOutPath</key>\n  <string>{log}</string>\n  <key>StandardErrorPath</key>\n  <string>{log}</string>\n ```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/whatsapp-fail/crates/node-host/src/service.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Generate a systemd user unit file.\npub fn generate_systemd_unit(\n    moltis_bin: &Path,\n    _config: &ServiceConfig,\n    log_path: &Path,\n) -> String {\n    let bin = moltis_bin.display();\n    let log = log_path.display();\n\n    let exec_args = format!(\"{bin} node run\");\n\n    format!(\n        r#\"[Unit]\nDescription=Moltis Node Host\nAfter=network-online.target\nWants=network-online.target\n\n[Service]\nType=simple\nExecStart={exec_args}\nRestart=on-failure\nRestartSec=10\nStandardOutput=append:{log}\nStandardError=append:{log}\nEnvironment=RUST_LOG=info\n\n[Install]\nWantedBy=default.target\n\"#\n    )\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Generate a systemd user unit file.\npub fn generate_systemd_unit(\n    moltis_bin: &Path,\n    config: &ServiceConfig,\n    log_path: &Path,\n) -> String {\n    let bin = moltis_bin.display();\n    let log = log_path.display();\n\n    let mut exec_parts = vec![format!(\n        \"{bin} node run --gateway-url {} --device-token {} --timeout {}\",\n        config.gateway_url, config.device_token, config.timeout\n    )];\n    if let Some(ref id) = config.node_id {\n        exec_parts.push(format!(\"--node-id {id}\"));\n    }\n    if let Some(ref name) = config.display_name {\n        exec_parts.push(format!(\"--name {name}\"));\n    }\n    if let Some(ref dir) = config.working_dir {\n        exec_parts.push(format!(\"--working-dir {dir}\"));\n    }\n    let exec_start = exec_parts.join(\" \");\n\n    format!(\n        r#\"[Unit]\nDescription=Moltis Node Host\nAfter=network-online.target\nWants=network-online.target\n\n[Service]\nType=simple\nExecStart={exec_start}\nRestart=on-failure\nRestartSec=10\nStandardOutput=append:{log}\nStandardError=append:{log}\nEnvironment=RUST_LOG=info\n\n[Install]\nWantedBy=default.target\n\"#\n    )\n}"
}

---
ID: 2912999487
File: crates/node-host/src/service.rs:218
Author: chatgpt-codex-connector[bot]
Body: **<sub><sub>![P1 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Remove unsupported flags from node service command**

This now injects `--gateway-url`/`--device-token` (and other config flags) into `moltis node run`, but `NodeAction::Run` only defines `--timeout` in `crates/cli/src/node_commands.rs`; Clap will reject the extra flags as unexpected args, so installed launchd/systemd services can fail immediately at startup instead of connecting. Keep `node run` reading `node.json` as before (or add matching `Run` flags) so service boot remains valid.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

---
ID: 2912999491
File: crates/gateway/src/channel.rs:311
Author: chatgpt-codex-connector[bot]
Body: **<sub><sub>![P1 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Reject invalid WhatsApp configs instead of persisting them**

The WhatsApp update path now swallows every `update_account_config` error and continues to persist the submitted config, which means malformed config payloads (deserialization/validation failures) are reported as success-with-warning and written to storage; the live session is unchanged and the account can later fail to start after restart because the stored config is invalid. Only non-fatal runtime update failures should be downgraded to warnings, while config parsing errors should still fail the request.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

---
ID: 2913000969
File: crates/node-host/src/service.rs:240
Author: greptile-apps[bot]
Body: **XML injection in launchd plist generation**

Config values (`gateway_url`, `device_token`, `display_name`, `working_dir`) are interpolated directly into XML `<string>` elements without escaping. If any value contains `&`, `<`, or `>`, the resulting plist will be invalid XML and `launchctl bootstrap` will fail silently or misparse the file.

For example, `gateway_url = "https://host/?a=1&b=2"` produces:
```xml
<string>https://host/?a=1&b=2</string>
```
which is malformed XML (`&b` is an unterminated entity reference).

The same issue applies to `moltis_bin` and `log_path` used elsewhere in the template; they were pre-existing, but the new fields make it more likely to trigger. Values should be XML-escaped before insertion:

```rust
fn xml_escape(s: &str) -> String {
    s.replace('&', "&amp;")
     .replace('<', "&lt;")
     .replace('>', "&gt;")
     .replace('"', "&quot;")
}
```

Then wrap each user-supplied value with `xml_escape(…)` before interpolating it.

---
ID: 2913001052
File: crates/node-host/src/service.rs:437
Author: greptile-apps[bot]
Body: **Unquoted values in systemd `ExecStart` break argument parsing**

`display_name` and `working_dir` are appended without quoting. Systemd applies POSIX-style word splitting to `ExecStart`, so a `display_name` like `"My Bot"` generates:

```
ExecStart=/usr/bin/moltis node run ... --name My Bot
```

which is parsed as `--name My` with `Bot` as an unrecognized positional argument, silently ignoring the display name (or causing a parse error).

Similarly, a `working_dir` path containing spaces will be split. Values with spaces must be quoted:

```rust
if let Some(ref name) = config.display_name {
    exec_parts.push(format!("--name \"{}\"", name.replace('"', "\\\"")));
}
if let Some(ref dir) = config.working_dir {
    exec_parts.push(format!("--working-dir \"{}\"", dir.replace('"', "\\\"")));
}
```

The same hardening should be applied to `gateway_url`, `device_token`, and `node_id` on line 426 for completeness.

---
ID: 2913001145
File: crates/whatsapp/src/handlers.rs:28
Author: greptile-apps[bot]
Body: **Write lock unnecessary for an atomic store**

`mirror_connected` acquires a **write** lock on the `AccountStateMap` just to call `AtomicBool::store`, which only requires `&self` (no mutation of the map structure itself). A **read** lock suffices here, improving concurrency since every other reader is blocked while this exclusive lock is held — including `probe_account` and `account_status` calls that happen frequently.

```suggestion
fn mirror_connected(accounts: &AccountStateMap, account_id: &str, connected: bool) {
    let map = accounts.read().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    if let Some(state) = map.get(account_id) {
        state.connected.store(connected, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }
}
```
```

> AGENT

Now I have all the context. Here's the plan:

**5 review comments, here's the assessment:**

1. **Codex: Unsupported CLI flags in service files** (P1) — **Valid and critical.** `NodeAction::Run` only accepts `--timeout`; it loads everything else from `node.json`. The plist/systemd generators should revert to just `moltis node run` (with optional `--timeout`), since `node.json` is already written by `install()`. The tests that assert config values appear in the plist/unit are wrong — they were written alongside the incomplete functions. Tests need to be fixed too.

2. **Codex: Persist invalid WhatsApp configs on failure** (P1) — **Valid.** Currently the hot-update error is swallowed as a warning and the (potentially invalid) config is still persisted. Fix: propagate `update_account_config` errors as a hard failure; only fall through to persist + warn if the error is specifically a "not running" scenario.

3. **Greptile: XML injection in launchd plist** — **Valid but low impact** after fix #1 (most user-supplied values will no longer be in the plist). Still worth adding `xml_escape` for `moltis_bin` and `log_path` which remain. Minimal effort.

4. **Greptile: Unquoted values in systemd ExecStart** — **Moot after fix #1** since config values won't be in ExecStart anymore. Only `moltis node run` + optional `--timeout <N>` (integer, no spaces).

5. **Greptile: Write lock unnecessary for AtomicBool store** — **Valid, easy fix.** Change `write()` to `read()` since `AtomicBool::store` only needs `&self`.

**Plan:**

1. Revert `generate_launchd_plist` and `generate_systemd_unit` to only emit `moltis node run` (+ `--timeout` if non-default), add `xml_escape` for safety
2. Fix tests to match — they should not assert config values appear in service files
3. In `channel.rs` `update()`, propagate `update_account_config` errors instead of swallowing; only add warning+persist if the account isn't running
4. Change `mirror_connected` from write lock to read lock

Want me to go ahead with these fixes?

  "old_string": "/// Generate a launchd plist XML string.\npub fn generate_launchd_plist(\n    moltis_bin: &Path,\n    config: &ServiceConfig,\n    log_path: &Path,\n) -> String {\n    let bin = moltis_bin.display();\n    let log = log_path.display();\n\n    let mut args = vec![\n        format!(\"    <string>{bin}</string>\"),\n        \"    <string>node</string>\".to_string(),\n        \"    <string>run</string>\".to_string(),\n        format!(\n            \"    <string>--gateway-url</string>\\n    <string>{}</string>\",\n            config.gateway_url\n        ),\n        format!(\n            \"    <string>--device-token</string>\\n    <string>{}</string>\",\n            config.device_token\n        ),\n        format!(\n            \"    <string>--timeout</string>\\n    <string>{}</string>\",\n            config.timeout\n        ),\n    ];\n    if let Some(ref id) = config.node_id {\n        args.push(format!(\n            \"    <string>--node-id</string>\\n    <string>{id}</string>\"\n        ));\n    }\n    if let Some(ref name) = config.display_name {\n        args.push(format!(\n            \"    <string>--name</string>\\n    <string>{name}</string>\"\n        ));\n    }\n    if let Some(ref dir) = config.working_dir {\n        args.push(format!(\n            \"    <string>--working-dir</string>\\n    <string>{dir}</string>\"\n        ));\n    }\n\n    let args_str = args.join(\"\\n\");",
  "new_string": "/// Escape a string for safe inclusion in XML text content.\nfn xml_escape(s: &str) -> String {\n    s.replace('&', \"&amp;\")\n        .replace('<', \"&lt;\")\n        .replace('>', \"&gt;\")\n        .replace('\"', \"&quot;\")\n}\n\n/// Generate a launchd plist XML string.\n///\n/// The service reads connection parameters from `node.json` (written by\n/// `moltis node add`), so only `--timeout` is passed as a CLI flag when\n/// it differs from the default.\npub fn generate_launchd_plist(\n    moltis_bin: &Path,\n    config: &ServiceConfig,\n    log_path: &Path,\n) -> String {\n    let bin = xml_escape(&moltis_bin.display().to_string());\n    let log = xml_escape(&log_path.display().to_string());\n\n    let mut args = vec![\n        format!(\"    <string>{bin}</string>\"),\n        \"    <string>node</string>\".to_string(),\n        \"    <string>run</string>\".to_string(),\n    ];\n    if config.timeout != default_timeout() {\n        args.push(format!(\n            \"    <string>--timeout</string>\\n    <string>{}</string>\",\n            config.timeout\n        ));\n    }\n\n    let args_str = args.join(\"\\n\");"
}

  "new_string": "/// Generate a systemd user unit file.\n///\n/// The service reads connection parameters from `node.json` (written by\n/// `moltis node add`), so only `--timeout` is passed as a CLI flag when\n/// it differs from the default.\npub fn generate_systemd_unit(\n    moltis_bin: &Path,\n    config: &ServiceConfig,\n    log_path: &Path,\n) -> String {\n    let bin = moltis_bin.display();\n    let log = log_path.display();\n\n    let mut exec_start = format!(\"{bin} node run\");\n    if config.timeout != default_timeout() {\n        exec_start.push_str(&format!(\" --timeout {}\", config.timeout));\n    }"

> AGENT

So `update_account_config` can fail with:
1. `serde_json::from_value` error — invalid config (deserialization failure)
2. `unknown_account` — account not running

The Codex comment is right: case 1 should be a hard error (don't persist bad config), case 2 is the "not running" case where we should warn but still persist.

Let me check the error type to see if we can distinguish them.

     3→/// Crate-wide result type for channel operations.
     4→pub type Result<T> = std::result::Result<T, Error>;
     5→
     6→/// Typed channel errors shared across channel traits.
     7→#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
     8→pub enum Error {
     9→    /// Input payload or parameter is invalid.
    10→    #[error("invalid channel input: {message}")]
    11→    InvalidInput { message: String },
    12→
    13→    /// A requested account ID is not registered.
    14→    #[error("unknown channel account: {account_id}")]
    15→    UnknownAccount { account_id: String },
    16→
    17→    /// Operation is currently unavailable (not configured/ready).
    18→    #[error("channel operation unavailable: {message}")]
    19→    Unavailable { message: String },
    20→
    21→    /// Wrapped source error from an external dependency.
    22→    #[error("channel operation failed: {context}: {source}")]
    23→    External {
    24→        context: String,
    25→        #[source]
    26→        source: Box<dyn StdError + Send + Sync>,
    27→    },
    28→
    29→    /// JSON (de)serialization failed.
    30→    #[error(transparent)]
    31→    SerdeJson(#[from] serde_json::Error),
    32→
    33→    /// Integer parsing failed.
    34→    #[error(transparent)]
    35→    ParseInt(#[from] std::num::ParseIntError),
    36→}
    37→
    38→impl Error {
    39→    /// Whether this error is transient and the operation may succeed if retried.
    40→    ///
    41→    /// - `External` errors are assumed retryable (network/transient failures).
    42→    /// - `Unavailable` errors are retryable (service may become ready).
    43→    /// - `UnknownAccount`, `InvalidInput`, parse, and serde errors are fatal.
    44→    #[must_use]
    45→    pub fn is_retryable(&self) -> bool {
    46→        matches!(self, Self::External { .. } | Self::Unavailable { .. })
    47→    }
    48→
    49→    #[must_use]
    50→    pub fn invalid_input(message: impl std::fmt::Display) -> Self {
    51→        Self::InvalidInput {
    52→            message: message.to_string(),
    53→        }
    54→    }
    55→
    56→    #[must_use]
    57→    pub fn unavailable(message: impl std::fmt::Display) -> Self {
    58→        Self::Unavailable {
    59→            message: message.to_string(),
    60→        }
    61→    }
    62→
    63→    #[must_use]
    64→    pub fn unknown_account(account_id: impl std::fmt::Display) -> Self {

    67→        }
    68→    }

    70→    #[must_use]
    71→    pub fn external(

    74→    ) -> Self {

> AGENT

Good. So the errors flow is: `UnknownAccount` from registry (not in index) or from plugin (not in accounts map), and `SerdeJson` from deserialization. The `UnknownAccount` case is the "not running" scenario — safe to warn and persist. Everything else should be a hard error.

> AGENT

There's `From<String> for ServiceError`, so `Err(e.to_string())` should work via the `?` operator. But the compiler says it expects `ServiceError`, not `String`. The issue is I used `return Err(e.to_string())` — the `From` impl means I need to use `?` or explicitly wrap. Let me just use `.into()`.

> AGENT

Pushed. All 5 review comments addressed:

1. **Service file generators** — reverted to only `moltis node run` + optional `--timeout`; `node run` reads `node.json` for connection params
2. **XML injection** — added `xml_escape()` for plist values
3. **Invalid config persistence** — `update_account_config` errors are now matched: `UnknownAccount` → warn + persist, all other errors → hard fail (no persist)
4. **Unquoted systemd values** — moot now since config values are no longer in `ExecStart`
5. **Write lock for AtomicBool** — `mirror_connected` now uses `read()` instead of `write()`

```
---
ID: 2913258233
File: crates/whatsapp/src/connection.rs:145
Body: **<sub><sub>![P1 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Signal shutdown only after the bot task actually exits**

`stop_account()` now waits on `shutdown.wait()`, but this flag is set unconditionally after the `select!` even when cancellation wins before the `handle` future completes; in that path the `JoinHandle` is dropped (Tokio detaches on drop), so the WhatsApp client task can keep running while `stop_account()` proceeds and removes the account. This reintroduces the sled lock race the graceful-shutdown path is trying to prevent, because a subsequent start can race an still-running previous bot.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

---
ID: 2913258243
File: crates/gateway/src/channel.rs:306
Body: **<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Restart WhatsApp when startup-bound config fields change**

This branch now hot-updates all WhatsApp config changes in place, but some fields are only applied during `start_connection` initialization (for example `otp_cooldown_secs` is baked into `OtpState::new(...)` and `update_account_config` only replaces `state.config`). As a result, successful `channel.update` calls can report updated config while live behavior still uses old startup-time values until a full restart.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.
```

    10→use crate::{
    11→    config::WhatsAppAccountConfig,

    13→    state::{AccountState, AccountStateMap, ShutdownState},
    14→};
    15→
    16→/// Start a WhatsApp connection for the given account.
    17→///
    18→/// Builds the `Bot` with a persistent sled store, registers the event handler,
    19→/// and spawns the event loop as a background tokio task. Session state persists
    20→/// across restarts so the user does not need to re-scan the QR code.
    21→pub async fn start_connection(
    22→    account_id: String,
    23→    config: WhatsAppAccountConfig,
    24→    accounts: AccountStateMap,
    25→    data_dir: std::path::PathBuf,
    26→    message_log: Option<Arc<dyn MessageLog>>,
    27→    event_sink: Option<Arc<dyn ChannelEventSink>>,
    28→) -> crate::Result<()> {
    29→    // Use persistent sled store at <data_dir>/whatsapp/<account_id>/.
    30→    let store_path = config
    31→        .store_path
    32→        .clone()
    33→        .unwrap_or_else(|| data_dir.join("whatsapp").join(&account_id));
    34→
    35→    info!(account_id = %account_id, path = %store_path.display(), "opening sled WhatsApp store");
    36→
    37→    let backend = Arc::new(
    38→        crate::sled_store::SledStore::open(&store_path).map_err(|e| crate::Error::Store {
    39→            message: format!("failed to open sled store at {}: {e}", store_path.display()),
    40→        })?,
    41→    );
    42→
    43→    let cancel = CancellationToken::new();
    44→    let cancel_clone = cancel.clone();
    45→    let shutdown = Arc::new(ShutdownState::new());
    46→    let shutdown_clone = Arc::clone(&shutdown);
    47→
    48→    // Build the bot.
    49→    let account_id_clone = account_id.clone();
    50→    let event_sink_clone = event_sink.clone();
    51→    let message_log_clone = message_log.clone();
    52→
    53→    // We need to create a temporary accounts ref for the state that will be
    54→    // populated after bot.build().
    55→    let state_ref: Arc<tokio::sync::OnceCell<Arc<AccountState>>> =
    56→        Arc::new(tokio::sync::OnceCell::new());
    57→    let state_ref_handler = Arc::clone(&state_ref);
    58→    let accounts_handler = Arc::clone(&accounts);
    59→
    60→    let mut bot = whatsapp_rust::bot::Bot::builder()
    61→        .with_backend(backend)
    62→        .with_transport_factory(
    63→            whatsapp_rust_tokio_transport::TokioWebSocketTransportFactory::new(),
    64→        )
    65→        .with_http_client(whatsapp_rust_ureq_http_client::UreqHttpClient::new())
    66→        .on_event(move |event, client| {
    67→            let state_ref = Arc::clone(&state_ref_handler);
    68→            let accounts = Arc::clone(&accounts_handler);
    69→            async move {
    70→                if let Some(state) = state_ref.get() {
    71→                    handlers::handle_event(event, client, Arc::clone(state), accounts).await;
    72→                }
    73→            }
    74→        })
    75→        .build()
    76→        .await
    77→        .map_err(|e| crate::Error::Whatsapp {
    78→            message: e.to_string(),
    79→        })?;
    80→
    81→    let client = bot.client();
    82→
    83→    // Create account state.
    84→    let otp_cooldown = config.otp_cooldown_secs;
    85→    let account_state = Arc::new(AccountState {
    86→        client: Arc::clone(&client),
    87→        account_id: account_id_clone.clone(),
    88→        config,
    89→        cancel: cancel_clone,
    90→        shutdown: Arc::clone(&shutdown),
    91→        message_log: message_log_clone,
    92→        event_sink: event_sink_clone,
    93→        latest_qr: std::sync::RwLock::new(None),
    94→        connected: std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool::new(false),
    95→        otp: std::sync::Mutex::new(crate::otp::OtpState::new(otp_cooldown)),
    96→        recent_sent_ids: std::sync::Mutex::new(std::collections::VecDeque::new()),
    97→    });
    98→
    99→    // Populate the OnceCell so the event handler can access state.
   100→    let _ = state_ref.set(Arc::clone(&account_state));
   101→
   102→    // Insert into the shared map.
   103→    {
   104→        let mut map = accounts.write().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
   105→        map.insert(account_id.clone(), AccountState {
   106→            client: Arc::clone(&client),
   107→            account_id: account_id.clone(),
   108→            config: account_state.config.clone(),
   109→            cancel: cancel.clone(),
   110→            shutdown: Arc::clone(&shutdown),
   111→            message_log: account_state.message_log.clone(),
   112→            event_sink: account_state.event_sink.clone(),
   113→            latest_qr: std::sync::RwLock::new(None),
   114→            connected: std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool::new(false),
   115→            otp: std::sync::Mutex::new(crate::otp::OtpState::new(otp_cooldown)),
   116→            recent_sent_ids: std::sync::Mutex::new(std::collections::VecDeque::new()),
   117→        });
   118→    }
   119→
   120→    // Spawn the event loop.
   121→    let account_id_task = account_id.clone();
   122→    tokio::spawn(async move {
   123→        tokio::select! {
   124→            result = bot.run() => {
   125→                match result {
   126→                    Ok(handle) => {
   127→                        tokio::select! {
   128→                            _ = handle => {
   129→                                info!(account_id = %account_id_task, "WhatsApp bot task completed");
   130→                            },
   131→                            _ = cancel.cancelled() => {
   132→                                info!(account_id = %account_id_task, "WhatsApp account cancelled");
   133→                            },
   134→                        }
   135→                    },
   136→                    Err(e) => {
   137→                        warn!(account_id = %account_id_task, error = %e, "WhatsApp bot failed to start");
   138→                    },
   139→                }
   140→            },
   141→            _ = cancel.cancelled() => {
   142→                info!(account_id = %account_id_task, "WhatsApp account cancelled before start");
   143→            },
   144→        }
   145→        shutdown_clone.mark_done();
   146→    });
   147→
   148→    Ok(())
   149→}
   150→
```

> AGENT

The issue: when cancellation wins in the `select!` at line 127-134, the `handle` (a `JoinHandle`) is dropped. Tokio detaches the task on drop, so the bot keeps running while `mark_done()` fires immediately. The fix: abort the handle explicitly before marking done.

Now **Fix 2 (P2): Startup-bound config fields**. The issue is that `otp_cooldown_secs` is baked into `OtpState::new()` at startup and `update_account_config` only replaces `state.config`. The simplest correct fix: in the `update_account_config` implementation in the WhatsApp plugin, also update `OtpState`'s cooldown when the config changes.

> AGENT

Both issues fixed and flagged on the PR:

1. **P1 shutdown race** — `JoinHandle` is now `pin!`ned, `abort()`ed on cancellation, and awaited before `mark_done()`, ensuring the sled store is fully released
2. **P2 startup-bound config** — `update_account_config` now updates `OtpState` cooldown via a new `set_cooldown()` method, so config changes take effect without restart

> AGENT

Now I see the full picture. Main has already partially fixed the same issues we fixed — the conflicts are from overlapping work. Main's approach:
- Always passes `--timeout` (not conditional)
- Uses `let args = [` (array) not `vec!`
- Uses `_config` in systemd (ignores config)
- Removed the old "omits optional" tests

Our branch adds:
- `xml_escape`
- Conditional `--timeout` only when non-default
- Extra test for XML escaping

The right resolution: take main's approach (always pass `--timeout` since it's simpler), keep our `xml_escape`, keep our extra tests where they add value. Let me resolve all conflicts.
